[0m$ [0msed -n '1,140p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs
#!/usr/bin/env node

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// MODULE: Frontier Admission
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// The frontier node store is append-only, one JSON object per line, and the
// LAST row per nodeId wins. Status has to survive a reduce and a resume, and
// neither the inbox (six fields) nor the registry (rebuilt from its inputs on
// every reduce) can carry it, so it lives here instead.
//
// This module is the store's only writer: seed, admit, prune and close all pass
// through it. The admission checks run in one fixed order so the workflow steps
// have a single deterministic implementation rather than prose the orchestrating
// model executes: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, monotone budget, acyclicity.
// The first failing check prunes the proposal with that check's name as the
// reason.
//
// Only an admitted node reaches the inbox, and the inbox is the only path that
// grows the counted question set mid-run. A pruned node is recorded in the store
// and nowhere else.
//
// A review run reads the same store with the review config instead: an admitted
// node is queued by the store alone, and relevance narrows to the review target,
// so no counted question is invented where the run answers none.
//
// Leaves propose; they never call this module.

'use strict';

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const NODE_STORE_FILE = 'frontier-nodes.jsonl';
const INBOX_FILE = 'inbox.jsonl';
const CONFIG_FILE = 'deep-research-config.json';
const REVIEW_CONFIG_FILE = 'deep-review-config.json';

// A target names the loop a call speaks for: which config file it reads and
// whether an admitted node queues a question.
const TARGETS = Object.freeze(['research', 'review']);

const DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH = 3;
const DEFAULT_MAX_CHILDREN_PER_NODE = 6;
const DEFAULT_MAX_FRONTIER_NODES = 10;

// The seed step decomposes a target into a documented number of depth-1 nodes:
// 3 to 6 sub-questions for research, 3 to 8 sub-targets for review. The root's
// children are those nodes, so its cap is the top of that range, never below the
// per-node cap. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so this cannot
// come from config alone.
const ROOT_CHILDREN_FLOOR = Object.freeze({ research: 6, review: 8 });

const NODE_STATUSES = Object.freeze([
  'open',
  'active',
  'answered',
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
  'pruned',
]);

// close cannot move a node back to the unopened or admitted state: seeding and
// admission own those, and a re-close is only meaningful for a lifecycle move.
const CLOSE_STATUSES = new Set([
  'active',
  'answered',
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
  'pruned',
]);

// A node closed with one of these never answered its question: the branch ran
// out, or it was dropped. The graph projection has to keep that visible.
const UNANSWERED_CLOSE_STATUSES = new Set([
  'blocked',
  'exhausted',
  'abstained',
]);

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. HELPERS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function isPlainObject(value) {
  return Boolean(value) && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function isNonEmptyString(value) {
  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '';
}

function hasScope(scope) {
  if (typeof scope === 'string') return scope.trim() !== '';
  return Array.isArray(scope) && scope.length > 0;
}

function normalizeIteration(value) {
  return Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0 ? value : 0;
}

function capValue(value, fallback) {
  return Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0 ? Math.floor(value) : fallback;
}

function assertTarget(target) {
  if (!TARGETS.includes(target)) {
    throw new Error(`unknown target: ${String(target)}; expected ${TARGETS.join(' or ')}`);
  }
  return target;
}

function appendJsonlRow(filePath, row) {
  fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(filePath), { recursive: true });
  fs.appendFileSync(filePath, `${JSON.stringify(row)}\n`, 'utf8');
}

function nodeDedupeKey(node) {
  return typeof node.dedupeKey === 'string' ? node.dedupeKey : dedupeKey(node.question);
}

function depthFor(proposal, nodes) {
  const parentId = isPlainObject(proposal) ? proposal.parentId : null;
  if (parentId === 'root') return 1;
  const parent = nodes.get(parentId);
  // A node with no resolvable parent was never placed on the graph. Zero keeps
  // that fact visible instead of inventing a depth the node never had.
  return parent && Number.isFinite(parent.depth) ? parent.depth + 1 : 0;
}

> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0msed -n '700,900p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs
return parsed;
}

function readSeedCandidates(filePath) {
  const parsed = readJsonFile(filePath, 'input file');
  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) throw new Error('--input for seed must hold a JSON array of candidates');
  return parsed;
}

function readProposalRows(filePath) {
  let content;
  try {
    content = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');
  } catch (error) {
    throw new Error(`cannot read input file ${filePath}: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`);
  }

  const proposals = [];
  for (const line of content.split('\n')) {
    const trimmed = line.trim();
    if (!trimmed) continue;
    let row;
    try {
      row = JSON.parse(trimmed);
    } catch {
      continue;
    }
    if (!isPlainObject(row) || row.type !== 'frontier_proposal') continue;
    proposals.push(isPlainObject(row.proposal) ? row.proposal : row);
  }
  return proposals;
}

function runCli(argv) {
  const args = parseArgs(argv);
  const command = args.command;

  if (command !== 'seed' && command !== 'admit' && command !== 'close'
    && command !== 'graph' && command !== 'block') {
    throw new Error(`unknown command: ${command === null ? '(none)' : command}; expected seed, admit, close, graph or block`);
  }

  const artifactDir = requireFlag(args, 'artifactDir', 'artifact-dir');
  const target = assertTarget(args.target === undefined ? 'research' : args.target);

  if (command === 'graph') {
    const output = requireFlag(args, 'output', 'output');
    return writeFrontierGraph({ artifactDir, output });
  }

  // The block is prompt text, so it goes to stdout verbatim rather than through
  // the JSON envelope the record-shaped commands return.
  if (command === 'block') {
    // A review leaf carries its node id, while the research block recovers the
    // node from the focus string the reducer wrote.
    const blockOptions = target === 'review'
      ? { artifactDir, nodeId: requireFlag(args, 'nodeId', 'node-id'), dimension: args.dimension, target }
      : { artifactDir, focus: requireFlag(args, 'focus', 'focus'), target };
    process.stdout.write(renderFrontierBlock(blockOptions));
    return undefined;
  }

  const iteration = parseIterationArg(args.iteration);
  const knownTexts = args.knownTexts === undefined ? [] : readKnownTexts(args.knownTexts);

  if (command === 'seed') {
    const candidates = readSeedCandidates(requireFlag(args, 'input', 'input'));
    return seedNodes({ artifactDir, iteration, candidates, knownTexts, target });
  }

  if (command === 'admit') {
    const proposals = readProposalRows(requireFlag(args, 'input', 'input'));
    return admitProposals({ artifactDir, iteration, proposals, knownTexts, target });
  }

  const nodeId = requireFlag(args, 'nodeId', 'node-id');
  const status = requireFlag(args, 'status', 'status');
  return closeNode({ artifactDir, nodeId, status, reason: args.reason === undefined ? null : args.reason, iteration });
}

function main(argv = process.argv.slice(2)) {
  try {
    const result = runCli(argv);
    if (result !== undefined) process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(result)}\n`);
    process.exit(0);
  } catch (error) {
    process.stderr.write(`frontier-admit: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}\n`);
    process.exit(2);
  }
}

if (require.main === module) {
  main();
}

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 6. EXPORTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

module.exports = {
  NODE_STATUSES,
  dedupeKey,
  readNodeStore,
  readCaps,
  scopeInsideReviewTarget,
  checkProposal,
  admitProposals,
  seedNodes,
  closeNode,
  renderFrontierBlock,
  buildFrontierGraphEvents,
  main,
};

const fs=require('fs');

for(const f of files){
  const t=fs.readFileSync(f,'utf8');
  const paths=[...new Set([...t.matchAll(/\.skilled\/[A-Za-z0-9_./{}$-]+/g)].map(m=>m[0]))];
  console.log('FILE',f,paths.length);
  for(const p of paths){
    const clean=p.replace(/[."',]+$/,'').replace(/\{[^}]+\}/g,'');
    if(clean.includes('{')) { console.log('  TEMPLATE',p); continue; }
    const ok=fs.existsSync(clean);
    if(!ok) console.log('  MISSING',p,'->',clean);
  }
}

[0m$ [0msed -n '1,120p' .skilled/commands/scripts/validate-command-references.cjs
#!/usr/bin/env node
// ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
// ║ COMPONENT: validate-command-references                                   ║
// ╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
// ║ PURPOSE: Referential-integrity checker for command-asset references.     ║
// ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
'use strict';

// Command-reference referential-integrity checker.
//
// WHY: the create / deep / design slash-command assets (_auto.yaml, _confirm.yaml)
// hard-code three kinds of live references — runtime-agent filenames
// ([runtime_agent_path]/<name>.md), literal skill-asset paths (.skilled/skills/**),
// and runtime-agent directory names — that command tooling validates for STRUCTURE
// but never for EXISTENCE. Retired agents, reorganized template paths, and phantom
// runtime directories therefore survive silently in the assets until a user hits a
// dead lookup at command runtime. This checker resolves every CONCRETE reference
// against disk and exits non-zero on the first unresolved one, so a broken reference
// fails fast in pre-flight / CI instead of mid-command.
//
// It deliberately skips anything parameterized: template placeholders ({...}, globs,
// $vars, and every reference other than the [runtime_agent_path] token that carries
// bracket/brace syntax), bare directory references (only file-shaped paths are
// resolved), and the legitimate .codex runtime-mirror tokens. Resolving those would
// false-positive on correct, intentionally-templated values.

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const fs = require('fs');
const os = require('os');
const path = require('path');

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');

// Real runtime-agent directories. An agent reference resolves if the file exists
// in ANY of these. AGENT_DIRS stays narrow because the wider runtimes mirror the
// same roster, so widening it would resolve nothing new.
const AGENT_DIRS = ['.skilled/agents', '.claude/agents', '.codex/agents'];
// A runtime-directory reference is valid only if it names a runtime that actually
// ships an agents tree. The point is to catch a phantom dir, not a runtime the repo
// grew later, so this lists every runtime with agents/ on disk.
const RUNTIME_DIR_ALLOWLIST = new Set(['.skilled', '.opencode', '.claude', '.codex', '.cursor', '.devin', '.pi', '.dsh', '.vibe']);
const COMMAND_INVENTORY_EXCLUDES = new Set(['assets', 'scripts', 'fixtures']);

// Command families are derived from the real command tree so a new family directory
// is covered the moment it ships assets, instead of drifting behind a hardcoded list.
function discoverFamilies(rootDir = REPO_ROOT) {
  const commandsRoot = path.join(rootDir, '.skilled', 'commands');
  if (!fs.existsSync(commandsRoot)) return [];
  return fs.readdirSync(commandsRoot, { withFileTypes: true })
    .filter((e) => e.isDirectory() && !COMMAND_INVENTORY_EXCLUDES.has(e.name))
    .map((e) => e.name)
    .filter((name) => fs.existsSync(path.join(commandsRoot, name, 'assets')))
    .sort();
}
const FAMILIES = discoverFamilies();

// Path token stops at YAML/prose delimiters and at any template metacharacter, so an
// interpolated reference (e.g. skills/{skill_name}/SKILL.md) truncates to a bare dir
// and is then dropped by the file-shaped filter below.
const SKILL_TOKEN = /\.(?:skilled|opencode)\/skills\/[^\s"'`,()\[\]{}<>$*|]+/g;
const AGENT_REF = /\[runtime_agent_path\]\/([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*\.md)/g;
const BARE_PHANTOM_AGENTS_DIR = /\.agents\//g;
const SCOPED_AGENTS_DIR = /(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*)\/agents\//g;
const COMMAND_TARGET = /\.(?:skilled|opencode)\/commands\/[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+\.(?:md|ya?ml|txt)/g;

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. HELPERS
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
function agentExists(fileName, rootDir = REPO_ROOT) {
  return AGENT_DIRS.some((d) => fs.existsSync(path.join(rootDir, d, fileName)));
}

// A concrete skill-asset reference is file-shaped (last segment carries an extension)
// and free of the template/anchor noise that would make on-disk resolution meaningless.
function normalizeSkillToken(raw) {
  let t = raw.split('#')[0]; // drop code anchors: foo.ts#symbol -> foo.ts
  t = t.replace(/[.,;:]+$/, ''); // drop trailing sentence punctuation
  const last = t.slice(t.lastIndexOf('/') + 1);
  if (!last.includes('.')) return null; // bare directory reference -> not resolved
  return t;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 4. CORE LOGIC
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
function extractViolations(file, rootDir = REPO_ROOT) {
  const rel = path.relative(rootDir, file);
  const lines = fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8').split('\n');
  const seen = new Set();
  const out = [];
  const add = (line, kind, ref, detail) => {
    const key = `${kind} ${ref}`;
    if (seen.has(key)) return;
    seen.add(key);
    out.push({ file: rel, line, kind, ref, detail });
  };

  lines.forEach((text, i) => {
    const lineNo = i + 1;
    let m;

    AGENT_REF.lastIndex = 0;
    while ((m = AGENT_REF.exec(text)) !== null) {
      const name = m[1];
      if (!agentExists(name, rootDir)) {
        add(lineNo, 'agent', `[runtime_agent_path]/${name}`, `no ${name} in ${AGENT_DIRS.join(', ')}`);
      }
    }

    SKILL_TOKEN.lastIndex = 0;
    while ((m = SKILL_TOKEN.exec(text)) !== null) {
      // A referenced skill path that is a runtime-generated artifact must not be
      // resolved against a clean checkout — it exists only while a command runs:
      //   - a match cut off at a shell glob wildcard is a truncated backup pattern
      //     (e.g. a `.sqlite.pre-<op>.*.bak` snapshot), never a concrete file.
[0m$ [0msed -n '1,80p' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md; echo "---REVIEW CMD"; sed -n '1,60p' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md

argument-hint: "<topic> [:auto|:confirm] [--spec-folder=PATH] [--max-iterations=N] [--executor=TYPE] [--concurrency=N] [--dry-run] [--restart] [--extend=N]"

# Deep Start Research Loop

Thin router for the deep-research loop. This command verifies the runtime agent, resolves setup and execution mode, loads the presentation contract, then executes the owned workflow YAML.

### MANDATORY INPUT GATE

- **DO NOT** dispatch any agent from this document.
- **ALL** agent dispatching is handled by the YAML workflow steps.
- **MARKDOWN OWNS SETUP**: resolve setup inputs here first, then hand off to YAML.
- **YAML START CONDITION**: do not load YAML until ALL required inputs are bound:
  - `research_topic`, `spec_folder`, `execution_mode`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `stop_policy`, `lineage_mode`
  - `dry_run` is normalized to `true` or `false`; absence means `false`

In `:auto` mode, an explicit `--restart` or `--lineage-mode=restart` flag (invocation form `--restart|--lineage-mode=restart`) is operator authorization to archive the resolved research packet before fan-out or phase init. Do not ask for a second confirmation; preserve rollback by moving the timestamped archive directory back to `research/` if needed.

## 1. ROUTER CONTRACT

Do not dispatch agents from this Markdown file. Agent dispatch, workflow steps, artifact-writing behavior, convergence detection, synthesis, and memory save behavior are owned by the workflow YAML assets.

Load the presentation contract before showing startup questions, dashboards, checkpoint text, success output, failure output, examples, or next-step prompts.


> **EXECUTION PROTOCOL -- READ FIRST**
>
> This command runs a structured YAML workflow. Do NOT dispatch agents from this document.
>
> **YOUR FIRST ACTION (HARD-BLOCK gate — do not skip):**
> 1. Run the Unified Setup Phase (BLOCKED gate) through the presentation contract and resolve:
>    - `research_topic`
>    - `spec_folder`
>    - `execution_mode`
>    - `maxIterations`
>    - `convergenceThreshold`
>    - `stop_policy` (default `convergence` unless `--stop-policy=max-iterations` is present)
>    - `dry_run` (default false unless `--dry-run` is present)
> 2. Load the workflow YAML from `assets/` only after all setup values are resolved: `deep-research.yaml`. The mode binds `execution_mode` to `auto` or `confirm`.
> 3. Execute the YAML workflow step by step using those resolved values
>
> This command is **general-agent based** — it orchestrates the deep-research loop. The Unified Setup Phase (the BLOCKED gate) is a HARD BLOCK; it may not be skipped.

For `:confirm` or no suffix, the consolidated setup prompt in the presentation contract MUST be the first visible response. For `:auto`, do not emit the consolidated setup prompt by default; use the auto setup resolution rules in the presentation contract and fail fast when required fields cannot be resolved.

> **Canonical mode syntax:** use attached command suffixes (`/deep:research:auto`, `/deep:research:confirm`).

> **Note:** Late-INIT `spec.md` detection and bounded `spec.md` mutations follow
> `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/spec-check-protocol.md`.
> Acquire `{spec_folder}/research/.deep-research.lock` before `folder_state` classification and keep `research/research.md` as the source of truth for any generated findings sync.
> Targeted strict validation after deep-research spec mutations uses the Node validation orchestrator.

### AUTONOMOUS EXECUTION DIRECTIVE (:auto)

> Read this first. It is an imperative to you, the executor — not background information.

You were invoked with the `:auto` suffix and a bound spec_folder and target in your message. Setup is already resolved.

- DO NOT emit the consolidated setup question (target/mode, topic, Spec Folder A/B/C/D, execution mode, max iterations, executor). The bound spec_folder is your write authority; do not ask which spec folder should own the writes.
- DO NOT halt for the repo-wide `CLAUDE.md` Gate-3 documentation gate. Under AUTONOMOUS execution with a validated prebound spec folder, Gate 3 is already satisfied by that folder (the prebound spec folder is your write authority).
- PROCEED immediately: load the workflow YAML with `execution_mode` bound to `auto` and execute it step by step.
- THE WORKFLOW DISPATCHES: the workflow YAML owns setup, the dispatch of the `deep-research` agent for each iteration, reducer sync, convergence checks, synthesis and all loop-level artifact writes. You do not dispatch from this document, and you do not read, edit, patch or run the loop over the target yourself. The `deep-research` leaf owns only its own single-iteration artifacts.
- ROUTE PROOF: dispatch through the workflow with `execution_mode` bound to `auto` and its prompt pack so `deep-research` writes each iteration state record with the route-proof fields present — `target_agent: "deep-research"`, `resolved_route`, `agent_definition_loaded: true`, and `mode: "research"`. A completed run whose iteration state records omit these fields is an incomplete delegation and does not pass.

This contract is your instruction set. The research target is the bound spec_folder or target named in your message, never this document.

[0m$ [0msed -n '120,240p' .skilled/commands/scripts/validate-command-references.cjs
//     (e.g. a `.sqlite.pre-<op>.*.bak` snapshot), never a concrete file.
      //   - a `.doctor-<route>.` dotfile is a lock/pid/state/log the doctor
      //     orchestrator writes and tears down within a single run.
      // The skip is unconditional (before the existence check) so a clean checkout
      // and a machine that happens to hold a leftover artifact behave identically.
      if (text.charAt(m.index + m[0].length) === '*') continue;
      const token = normalizeSkillToken(m[0]);
      if (!token) continue;
      if (/^\.doctor-[a-z0-9-]+\./.test(path.basename(token))) continue;
      // A path under the gitignored runtime state root is written while a command runs, so a clean
      // checkout never holds it. The tracked README under it stays checked.
      if (/^\.skilled\/skills\/\.state\//.test(token) && path.basename(token) !== 'README.md') continue;
      // The deep-loop graph databases are gitignored and built on first use, so a clean checkout
      // or a fresh worktree holds none of them.
      if (/^\.skilled\/skills\/system-deep-loop\/runtime\/database\/[^/]+\.sqlite$/.test(token)) continue;
      if (!fs.existsSync(path.join(rootDir, token))) {
        add(lineNo, 'skill-asset', token, 'path does not exist on disk');
      }
    }

    BARE_PHANTOM_AGENTS_DIR.lastIndex = 0;
    if (BARE_PHANTOM_AGENTS_DIR.test(text)) {
      add(lineNo, 'runtime-dir', '.agents/', 'phantom runtime directory (not a real agent dir)');
    }

    SCOPED_AGENTS_DIR.lastIndex = 0;
    while ((m = SCOPED_AGENTS_DIR.exec(text)) !== null) {
      if (!RUNTIME_DIR_ALLOWLIST.has(m[1])) {
        add(lineNo, 'runtime-dir', `${m[1]}/agents/`, `not an allowlisted runtime dir (${[...RUNTIME_DIR_ALLOWLIST].join(', ')})`);
      }
    }
  });

  return out;
}

function expandTargets(inputs) {
  const files = [];
  for (const raw of inputs) {
    const abs = path.resolve(process.cwd(), raw);
    if (!fs.existsSync(abs)) continue;
    if (fs.statSync(abs).isDirectory()) {
      for (const f of fs.readdirSync(abs)) {
        if (f.endsWith('.yaml')) files.push(path.join(abs, f));
      }
    } else {
      files.push(abs);
    }
  }
  return files;
}

function defaultTargets() {
  const files = [];
  for (const fam of FAMILIES) {
    const dir = path.join(REPO_ROOT, '.skilled', 'commands', fam, 'assets');
    if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) continue;
    for (const f of fs.readdirSync(dir)) {
      if (f.endsWith('.yaml')) files.push(path.join(dir, f));
    }
  }
  return files;
}

function scan(targets) {
  return targets.flatMap((target) => extractViolations(target));
}

// The prompt-sync source contract excludes non-command support directories and
// non-invocable Markdown. Keeping that inventory here gives adapters one shared
// source for discovery while the sync CLI remains the exact live parity gate.
function commandSourceInventory(rootDir = REPO_ROOT) {
  // The command tree sits under .skilled, or under .opencode in a checkout that predates
  // the move, and each source path keeps the spelling of the root it was found under.
  const commandsRoot = ['.skilled', '.opencode']
    .map((rootName) => path.join(rootDir, rootName, 'commands'))
    .find((candidate) => fs.existsSync(candidate));
  if (!commandsRoot) return [];
  const sources = [];
  const walk = (directory) => {
    for (const entry of fs.readdirSync(directory, { withFileTypes: true })) {
      const absolute = path.join(directory, entry.name);
      if (entry.isDirectory()) {
        if (!COMMAND_INVENTORY_EXCLUDES.has(entry.name)) walk(absolute);
        continue;
      }
      if (!entry.isFile() || !entry.name.endsWith('.md')) continue;
      if (entry.name === 'README.md' || entry.name.endsWith('.contract.md')) continue;
      sources.push(path.relative(rootDir, absolute).split(path.sep).join('/'));
    }
  };
  walk(commandsRoot);
  return sources.sort();
}

function lineNumber(text, offset) {
  return text.slice(0, offset).split('\n').length;
}

function extractCommandTargets(text) {
  const targets = [];
  COMMAND_TARGET.lastIndex = 0;
  let match;
  while ((match = COMMAND_TARGET.exec(text)) !== null) {
    targets.push({ target: match[0], line: lineNumber(text, match.index) });
  }
  return targets;
}

+### Expansive Depth Mode
+
+- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which renders the same bytes as before.
+- `step_frontier_seed` writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`, after `step_create_config`, or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run.
+- `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Admitted nodes land in `research/inbox.jsonl` with `origin: "frontier"`.
+- Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>`.
+- After the `minIterations` floor an open or active node turns a soft stop into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never creates a stop and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`.
+- A fan-out run deals the seeded depth-1 nodes round-robin across lineages and merges them back under a partition guard, so no dealt node goes unreported.
+
 ### Invocation Contract
 
 This skill is invoked exclusively through `/deep:research:auto` or `/deep:research:confirm` -- the command YAML owns state, dispatch, convergence, and synthesis. Never simulate the loop with ad hoc shell dispatch, nested CLI loops, direct `@deep-research` Task dispatch, `/tmp` prompt files, or state outside the resolved local research packet.
@@ -123,7 +132,9 @@ Runtime-supported lifecycle modes:
 | `resume` | Continue the active lineage and append a typed `resumed` JSONL event |
 | `restart` | Archive the existing research tree, mint a fresh `sessionId`, increment `generation`, and append a typed `restarted` event |
 
-Deferred modes `fork` and `completed-continue` are reserved but not runtime-supported.
+`completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`: it reopens a completed run in the same session and generation, raises the iteration cap to the runs so far plus N, and appends a typed `run_extended` event.
+
+The deferred mode `fork` is reserved but not runtime-supported.

 <!-- Note: Task is for the command executor (loop management); @deep-review agent is LEAF-only (no Task). No WebFetch: review is code-only. -->
@@ -232,6 +232,16 @@ The stuck count and the consecutive failure count are rebuilt from the state log
 
 Each pass also compares `maxDurationMinutes` with the config `createdAt`. When the limit is reached the loop skips the dispatch and goes to synthesis with stop reason `maxDurationReached`.
 
+### Expansive Depth Mode
+
+- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
+- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. Both are locked against the optimizer.
+- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
+- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
+- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
+- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
+- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size `--max-iterations` so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.
+

const fs=require('fs'),crypto=require('crypto');
for (const f of ['.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-research.contract.markdown','.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-review.contract.markdown']) {
  const t=fs.readFileSync(f,'utf8');

  const j=JSON.parse(m[1]);
  console.log('FILE',f);
  let bad=0;
  for (const s of j.sourceDigests) {
    let actual;
    try { actual=crypto.createHash('sha256').update(fs.readFileSync(s.path)).digest('hex'); } catch(e){ console.log('  MISSING',s.path); bad++; continue; }
    if (actual!==s.sha256) { console.log('  STALE',s.path,'\n    header',s.sha256,'\n    actual',actual); bad++; }
  }
  console.log('  stale/missing:',bad,'of',j.sourceDigests.length);
  const body=t.split('GENERATED_COMMAND_CONTRACT_HEADER_END -->\n')[1]||'';
  const bodyDigest=crypto.createHash('sha256').update(body).digest('hex');
  console.log('  compiledBodyDigest',j.compiledBodyDigest===bodyDigest?'OK':'MISMATCH',j.compiledBodyDigest,bodyDigest);
}

# v2.1.6.0, Research Runs Use the Configured Claude Config Dir

A research run on Claude ignored the config dir the operator set. It ran under whichever account the shell had. Research now honors the configured dir.

function findToolAllowlistOverflow(command, contractText) {
  const definition = getCommandDefinition(command);
  const commandTools = parseCommandAllowedTools(readText(definition.commandPath));
  const overflows = [];

  for (const tool of parseContractAllowedTools(contractText)) {
    if (!commandTools.has(tool)) overflows.push(tool);
  }

  return overflows;
}

function extractRenderBlock(contractText, blockName) {
  const startMarker = `<!-- START renderBlocks.${blockName} -->`;
  const endMarker = `<!-- END renderBlocks.${blockName} -->`;
  const start = contractText.indexOf(startMarker);
  const end = contractText.indexOf(endMarker, start + startMarker.length);
  if (start === -1 || end === -1) return null;
  return contractText.slice(start + startMarker.length, end);
}

function renderBlockPayload(blockText) {
  return blockText
    .replace(/^\s*~~~[^\n]*\n/, '')
    .replace(/\n~~~\s*$/, '')
    .trim();
}

function findUnresolvedMarkers(contractText) {
  const issues = [];
  const markers = Array.from(new Set(contractText.match(PLACEHOLDER_PATTERN) || []));
  for (const marker of markers) {
    issues.push(`unresolved placeholder token ${marker}`);
  }

  for (const blockName of REQUIRED_RENDER_BLOCKS) {
    const block = extractRenderBlock(contractText, blockName);
    if (block === null) {
      issues.push(`missing renderBlocks.${blockName}`);
      continue;
    }
    if (!renderBlockPayload(block)) issues.push(`empty renderBlocks.${blockName}`);
  }

  return issues;
}

def _json_hash(payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Return a stable SHA-256 hash for compact canonical JSON."""
    canonical = json.dumps(payload, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    return f"sha256:{digest}"


def _load_mode_registry_projection(classes: Set[str]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Load projection entries from the parent-skill mode registry."""
    with open(MODE_REGISTRY_PATH, "r", encoding="utf-8") as registry_file:
        registry = json.load(registry_file)

    entries: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    for mode in registry.get("modes", []):
        if not isinstance(mode, dict):
            continue
        routing = mode.get("advisorRouting")
        if not isinstance(routing, dict):
            continue
        routing_class = routing.get("routingClass")
        if routing_class not in classes:
            continue
        legacy_id = routing.get("legacyAdvisorId")
        workflow_mode = mode.get("workflowMode")
        if not isinstance(legacy_id, str) or not isinstance(workflow_mode, str):
            raise ValueError(f"Mode registry entry missing legacy id or workflowMode: {mode!r}")
        aliases = routing.get("legacyAliases", [])
        if not isinstance(aliases, list) or not all(isinstance(alias, str) for alias in aliases):
            raise ValueError(f"Mode registry entry has invalid legacyAliases: {mode!r}")
        entries.append({
            "legacyAdvisorId": legacy_id,
            "workflowMode": workflow_mode,
            "routingClass": routing_class,
            "advisorDefaultMode": routing.get("advisorDefaultMode") is True,
            "legacyAliases": aliases,
        })

#!/usr/bin/env node
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// MODULE: Render Command Contract
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
'use strict';

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 1. IMPORTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const crypto = require('node:crypto');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');

const {
  canonicalizeCommand,
  resolveInjectionMode,
} = require('../../shared/rollout/resolve-injection-mode.cjs');
const { checkCommand } = require('./check-contract-drift.cjs');

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 2. CONSTANTS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
const WORKSPACE_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..', '..');
const MANIFEST_PATH = '.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/manifest.jsonl';

const COMMANDS = {
  'deep/review': {
    slug: 'deep_review',
    legacyBodyPath: '.skilled/commands/deep/assets/legacy/deep-review.body.markdown',
    compiledContractPath: '.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-review.contract.markdown',
  },
  'deep/research': {
    slug: 'deep_research',
    legacyBodyPath: '.skilled/commands/deep/assets/legacy/deep-research.body.markdown',
    compiledContractPath: '.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-research.contract.markdown',
  },
};

// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// 3. HELPERS
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
function absolutePath(sourcePath) {
  return path.resolve(WORKSPACE_ROOT, sourcePath);
}

function sha256(input) {
  return crypto.createHash('sha256').update(input).digest('hex');
}

function readBuffer(sourcePath) {
  return fs.readFileSync(absolutePath(sourcePath));
}

function getCommandDefinition(command) {
  const canonicalCommand = canonicalizeCommand(command);
  const definition = COMMANDS[canonicalCommand];
  if (!definition) {
    const supported = Object.keys(COMMANDS).join(', ');
    throw new Error(`Unsupported command ${command}. Supported commands: ${supported}`);
  }
  return { command: canonicalCommand, ...definition };
}

function passthroughArgsText(argv) {
  if (argv.length === 0) return '';
  if (argv.length === 1) return argv[0];
  return argv.join(' ');
}

function resolveMode(command) {
  return resolveInjectionMode(command);
}

function assertCompiledContractFresh(definition, check = checkCommand) {
  const drift = check(definition.command);
  if (drift.failures.length > 0) {
    const classes = [...new Set(drift.failures.map((failure) => failure.class))].join(', ');
    throw new Error(
      `Refusing stale compiled contract for ${definition.command}: ${classes}. `
        + 'Re-run compile-command-contracts.cjs --write.',
    );
  }
}

function renderPayload(definition, mode) {
  const legacyBody = readBuffer(definition.legacyBodyPath);

  if (mode === 'fallback') return legacyBody;
  if (mode === 'fix') {
    assertCompiledContractFresh(definition);
    const compiledContract = readBuffer(definition.compiledContractPath);
    return Buffer.concat([compiledContract, legacyBody]);
  }
  throw new Error(`Unsupported injection mode ${mode} for ${definition.command}`);
}

// The command reaches the executor as rendered markdown; the raw invocation message
// (target, :auto suffix, spec_folder, topic, flags) arrives only via this prelude's
// argv and must be echoed back, or the model never sees it and re-asks for setup.
// This mirrors the memory-search command's ARGS_PRESENT/QUERY surface.
function buildInvocationPrefix(argsText) {
  const present = argsText.trim().length > 0;
  const lines = [
    '<!-- INVOCATION MESSAGE (the user input for this run) -->',
    `ARGS_PRESENT=${present}`,
  ];
  if (present) {
    lines.push('MESSAGE:', argsText, '');
    lines.push(
      'Bind setup from the MESSAGE above — the target, the `:auto`/`:confirm` suffix, `--spec-folder`, the research topic, and other flags are there. When ARGS_PRESENT=true you MUST proceed on that MESSAGE now: do NOT ask the setup question, and do NOT treat a populated MESSAGE as empty.',
    );
  } else {
    lines.push('MESSAGE: (none supplied)', '');
    lines.push('ARGS_PRESENT=false: no invocation message was supplied; follow the command body below for setup routing.');
  }
  lines.push('', '<!-- END INVOCATION MESSAGE -->', '');
  return Buffer.from(lines.join('\n'), 'utf8');
}

function buildManifestRow(definition, mode, argsText, output) {

20:Most of the folder is a link. `skills`, `agents`, `hooks`, `bin`, `scripts`, `repo-rules` and `manual-testing-playbook` each point at a directory under `.skilled/`. Inside `commands/`, every top-level entry of `.skilled/commands/` arrives as a link except the git-ignored `assets` folder. A private `local-*` entry is neither written nor pruned, so an operator checkout can carry one as a link, and `goal-opencode.md` is the only real file there. A link holds no content of its own, so the file behind it is the only copy and an edit through the link lands on the source.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:469:On a fan-out run the depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, `frontier_scope:` binds each lineage's node ids beside `review_focus:` in its prompt, and the partition is recorded as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. Each lineage's store is written before it spawns, the merge returns a merged `frontier`, and a partition guard names any dealt node no lineage reports. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so 50% of each lineage's `maxIterations` should reach `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`; below that the depth-1 band cannot fund the nodes it was dealt.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md:243:- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. .skilled/commands/deep/review.md:97:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (record the pilot range SHA; merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` for the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

[0m$ [0msed -n '96,112p' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md; echo "---review"; sed -n '90,104p' .skilled/commands/deep/review.md
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default, on the ordinary legal-stop path. `divergent` sends a `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered` stop into a one-round three-seat Council pivot instead of ending the run, at proportionally higher cost. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the `research/resource-map.md` write; `research.md` still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--restart` | no value | Shorthand that selects `restart`: opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. |
| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
---review

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default. `divergent` routes an eligible convergence stop into a Council pivot rather than ending the run. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (record the pilot range SHA; merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` for the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the resource-map write; the review report still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--review-focus` | `"TEXT"` | Optional operator focus that every iteration covers in addition to its dimension, in single-executor and fan-out runs alike. It is cut to one line of at most 1000 characters, stored as `reviewFocus` in the config and shown in the review report. |
| `--restart`, `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing; `restart`, which bare `--restart` selects, opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `review/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |

[0m$ [0msed -n '960,1060p' .skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime/scripts/skill_graph_compiler.py; echo "---parse sources"; grep -n "description.json\|SKILL.md\|Keywords:" .skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime/scripts/skill_graph_compiler.py | head -30
skill_id = data.get("skill_id", folder_name)
        family = data.get("family", "unknown")

        # Build families
        if family not in families:
            families[family] = []
        families[family].append(skill_id)

        # Build sparse adjacency
        edges = normalized_edges(data)
        skill_adj: Dict[str, dict] = {}

        # Keep core sparse edge groups in compiled output.
        for edge_type in ("depends_on", "enhances", "siblings", "prerequisite_for"):
            edge_list = edges.get(edge_type, [])
            if edge_list:
                targets = {}
                for edge in edge_list:
                    target = edge.get("target")
                    weight = edge.get("weight", 0.0)
                    if target and weight > 0.0:
                        targets[target] = weight
                if targets:
                    skill_adj[edge_type] = targets

        for affordance in normalized_affordances(data.get("derived")):
            for edge in affordance.get("edges", []):
                edge_type = edge.get("edge_type")
                if edge_type not in ("depends_on", "enhances", "siblings", "prerequisite_for"):
                    continue
                target = edge.get("target")
                weight = edge.get("weight", 0.0)
                if target in known_skill_ids and isinstance(weight, (int, float)) and weight > 0.0:
                    skill_adj.setdefault(edge_type, {})[target] = weight

        if skill_adj:
            adjacency[skill_id] = skill_adj

        # Record directional conflict declarations for the reciprocity pass.
        for edge in edges.get("conflicts_with", []):
            target = edge.get("target")
            if target:
                declared_conflicts.setdefault(skill_id, set()).add(target)

    # Now emit only mutually-declared conflict pairs.
    conflicts: List[List[str]] = []
    seen_conflicts: Set[tuple] = set()
    for skill_id, targets in declared_conflicts.items():
        for target in targets:
            if skill_id not in declared_conflicts.get(target, set()):
                # Defensive: upstream validation should have caught this.
                continue
            pair = tuple(sorted([skill_id, target]))
            if pair not in seen_conflicts:
                seen_conflicts.add(pair)
                conflicts.append(list(pair))

    signals = {}
    for folder_name, _, data in all_metadata:
        skill_id = data.get("skill_id", folder_name)
        sigs = list(data.get("intent_signals", []))
        for affordance in normalized_affordances(data.get("derived")):
            sigs.extend(affordance.get("derived_triggers", []))
        if sigs:
            signals[skill_id] = list(dict.fromkeys(sigs))

    # Sort family members
    for family in families:
        families[family] = sorted(families[family])

    hub_skills = compute_hub_skills(adjacency)

    # Normalize and embed topology warnings so that `health_check()` can
    # report degraded state without re-running validation.
    serialized_warnings: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
    if topology_warnings:
        for category, messages in topology_warnings.items():
            if not messages:
                continue
            # Copy to break aliasing; sort for stable output.
            serialized_warnings[str(category)] = sorted(str(m) for m in messages)

    return {
        "schema_version": COMPILED_SCHEMA_VERSION,
        "generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        "skill_count": len(all_metadata),
        "families": dict(sorted(families.items())),
        "adjacency": dict(sorted(adjacency.items())),
        "signals": dict(sorted(signals.items())),
        "conflicts": sorted(conflicts),
        "hub_skills": hub_skills,
        "topology_warnings": dict(sorted(serialized_warnings.items())),
    }

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * Carry a hub router's declared vocabulary into the stage the advisor reads.
 *
 * usage: generate-router-intent-signals.cjs --check|--write [--hub <id>] [--json]
 *
 * Routing runs in two stages. The advisor scores a hub's graph-metadata.json
 * intent_signals to choose a hub; that hub's ROUTER.md then chooses a mode. The
 * two vocabularies are not meant to be identical, because a bare common word
 * belongs in the second and would over-trigger in the first.
 *
 * They are not meant to be disjoint either. Exact membership in intent_signals
 * is what fires the explicit_author lane, and that lane both raises the score and
 * emits the evidence entries that pull uncertainty under the surfacing gate. A
 * phrase a router advertises while the advisor has never heard of it is a
 * capability the fleet has and cannot be asked for.
 *
 * So every multi-word phrase a router declares is carried up. Each phrase this
 * writer adds is also recorded in `intent_signals_generated`, the provenance
 * list: an entry there that no router declares any more is an orphan, meaning
 * the router renamed or dropped a phrase the advisor still advertises. `--check`
 * names and counts orphans and fails, and `--write` removes them from both
 * lists. `intent_signals_keep` pins a generated phrase that should outlive its
 * router entry. An `intent_signals` entry no router declares is hand-curated,
 * so it is never treated as an orphan. A phrase a router does declare is
 * recorded on the hub's first `--write`, so a later router rename prunes it
 * unless `intent_signals_keep` pins it. Single words are left behind
 * deliberately, since they are the ones that would over-trigger.
 */

'use strict';

const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');

const REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..', '..');
const SKILLS = path.join(REPO_ROOT, '.skilled', 'skills');

function hubs() {
  return fs.readdirSync(SKILLS, { withFileTypes: true })
    .filter((e) => e.isDirectory())
    .map((e) => e.name)
    // A private skill belongs to one checkout, so this writer must never open or rewrite it.
    .filter((name) => !name.startsWith('local-'))
    .filter((name) => fs.existsSync(path.join(SKILLS, name, 'graph-metadata.json')))
    .sort();
}

function isMultiWordPhrase(phrase) {
  return phrase.includes(' ')
    && /^[a-z0-9]/i.test(phrase)
    && !/[{}[\]]/.test(phrase)
    && !/^(weight|keywords)$/i.test(phrase);
}

// The same extraction the reach check probes with. Keeping one definition means
// the gate and the generator can never disagree about what a router declared.
function declaredPhrases(hub) {
  // A hub declares stage-one vocabulary in ROUTER.md. A router-less skill
  // declares it in the keyword list its SKILL.md already carries.
  const hasHubRouter = fs.existsSync(path.join(SKILLS, hub, 'hub-router.json'));
  const sourcePath = path.join(SKILLS, hub, hasHubRouter ? 'ROUTER.md' : 'SKILL.md');
  if (!fs.existsSync(sourcePath)) return [];

  const text = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, 'utf8');
  if (!hasHubRouter) {
    const keywords = /<!--\s*Keywords:\s*([\s\S]*?)\s*-->/i.exec(text);
    if (!keywords) return [];
    return [...new Set(keywords[1].split(',').map((phrase) => phrase.trim()).filter(isMultiWordPhrase))];
  }

  const block = /INTENT_SIGNALS\s*=\s*\{([^\n]*(?=\}[ \t]*$)|[\s\S]*?(?=\n\}))/m.exec(text);
  if (!block) return [];
  const found = [...block[1].matchAll(/"([^"]{3,60})"/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
  return [...new Set(found.filter(isMultiWordPhrase))];
}

/**
 * Plan one hub's vocabulary change.
 *
 * `missing` is a phrase the router declares that `intent_signals` lacks.
 * `orphans` is the reverse defect that provenance makes visible: a phrase an
 * earlier `--write` recorded in `intent_signals_generated` that the router no
 * longer declares, unless `intent_signals_keep` pins it. An entry no router
 * declares is hand-curated: it never appears in `intent_signals_generated`, so
 * it can never be an orphan. A phrase a router does declare is recorded on the
 * hub's first `--write`, so a later router rename prunes it unless kept.
 */
function plan(hub) {
  const metaPath = path.join(SKILLS, hub, 'graph-metadata.json');

.cursor/commands/deep-review.md:97:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (record the pilot range SHA; merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` for the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
.cursor/commands/deep-research.md:104:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open.
